and consider alternative, potentially more appropriate, functional relationships linking the presence and severity components. Despite these limitations, the models successfully identified biologically plausible and clinically relevant protective and risk factors for dental fluorosis.

Supplementary information for “Analyzing Zero-Inflated Clustered Longitudinal Ordinal Outcomes Using GEE-Type Models With an Application to Dental Fluorosis Studies”

S1
Covariates in Iowa Fluoride Study (IFS) data

S1.1
Categorical predictors

•

Tooth: tooth locations 7,8,9,10, coded by the universal numbering system. Tooth 7 (upper right, lateral incisor) is treated as the reference level. Teeth 8 and 9 are the two maxillary central incisors (right and left, respectively), and tooth 10 is the upper left, lateral incisor.

•

Zone: Tooth zones include the cervical third (C), middle third (M), incisal third (I) of the buccal surface, and the incisal edge (E), measured on each of the four maxillary incisors (reference: zone C).

S1.2
Continuous Covariates

•

Dental_age: patient age at dental examination. It is the child’s age in years at the time of the dental examination associated with measurement occasion t. In practice, the means of actual
dental examination ages are slightly higher than the scheduled ages of 9, 13 and 17, and age is centered by the scheduled time. For instance, if the child’s dental appointment is 2 months after the scheduled time, the dental age would be reported as
2/12=0.17
.

•

Total_mgFPerDay: total daily fluoride intake (mg) determined by combining fluoride intake from water, other beverages and selected foods, ingested toothpaste, and dietary fluoride supplements before permanent teeth eruption. The value was computed through an AUC (area under the curve) trapezoidal method using all available
data measured approximately every 6 months for ages of 0-5.

•

SugarAddedBeverageOzPerDay: daily sugar-added beverage intake (oz.) was computed by an AUC trapezoidal method using all available data measured approximately every 6 months for ages of
0-5.

•

BrushingFrequencyPerDay: average of the tooth brushing frequencies per day was
reported approximately every 6 months for ages of 0-5 and combined using an AUC
trapezoidal approach.

•

Avg_homeppm: fluoride exposure from home tap water. It is the average home tap water fluoride level (ppm) reported for all returned
questionnaires approximately every 6 months for ages of 0-5, combined through a
trapezoidal AUC method.

•

Prop_DentalAppt: proportion of times a dental visit was reported for approximately 6-month intervals for ages of 0-5.

•

Prop_FluorideTreatment: proportion of times a professional dental fluoride treatment was reported in approximately 6-month intervals for ages of 0-5.

S2
Components of the Presence GEE

\displaystyle\mu_{P,tijk}

\displaystyle=\frac{\exp(\alpha+ \boldsymbol{x_{P,tijk}'\beta})}{1+\exp(\alpha+ \boldsymbol{x_{P,tijk}'\beta})}

(1)

\displaystyle\mu_{P,tijk}

\displaystyle=\big(\mu_{P,i,111},\mu_{P,i,112},\cdots,\mu_{P,i,TJK}\big)^{T}

(2)

\displaystyle\partialderivative{\mu_{P,tijk}}{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\frac{\exp(\alpha+ \boldsymbol{x_{P,tijk}'\beta})}{1+\exp(\alpha+ \boldsymbol{x_{P,tijk}'\beta})}-\Bigg(\frac{\exp(\alpha+ \boldsymbol{x_{P,tijk}'\beta})}{1+\exp(\alpha+ \boldsymbol{x_{P,tijk}'\beta})}\Bigg)^{2}=\mu_{P,tijk}(1-\mu_{P,tijk})

(3)

\displaystyle\partialderivative{\mu_{P,tijk}}{\beta_g}

\displaystyle=\mu_{P,tijk}(1-\mu_{P,tijk})x_{tijk,g}\text{, where }g=1,\cdots q

(4)

\displaystyle\partialderivative{\mu_{i}}{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\partialderivative{\mu_{i, 111}}{\alpha},\partialderivative{\mu_{i, 112}}{\alpha},\cdots,\partialderivative{\mu_{i, T J K}}{\alpha}\bigg)^{T}

(5)

\displaystyle\partialderivative{\mu_{ti}}{\beta_g}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\partialderivative{\mu_{ti11}}{\beta_g},\partialderivative{\mu_{ti12}}{\beta_g},\cdots,\partialderivative{\mu_{TiJK}}{\beta_g}\bigg)^{T},\text{for }g=1,\cdots,q

(6)

\displaystyle\bm{Y_{ti}}

\displaystyle=(Y_{ti11},Y_{ti12},\cdots,Y_{TiJK})^{T},

(7)

where the indicators
Y_{P,tijk}=1
if the corresponding FRI Score is non-zero (in
7
).

S3
Components of the Severity GEE

We present the derivations corresponding to
L=3
, as is the case for the real IFS data. Setting
\gamma=1
corresponds to the case of separate modeling severity piece.

\displaystyle\mu_{S,{S,tijk},1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\exp(\alpha_{1} + \gamma(\boldsymbol{x_{S,tijk}'\beta}))}{1+\exp(\alpha_{1} + \gamma(\boldsymbol{x_{S,tijk}'\beta}))}

(8)

\displaystyle\mu_{S,tijk,2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\exp(\alpha_{2} + \gamma(\boldsymbol{x_{S,tijk}'\beta}))}{1+\exp(\alpha_{2} + \gamma(\boldsymbol{x_{S,tijk}'\beta}))}-\frac{\exp(\alpha_{1} + \gamma(\boldsymbol{x_{S,tijk}'\beta}))}{1+\exp(\alpha_{1} + \gamma(\boldsymbol{x_{S,tijk}'\beta}))}

(9)

\displaystyle\mu_{S,tijk,3}

\displaystyle=1-\frac{\exp(\alpha_{2} + \gamma(\boldsymbol{x_{S,tijk}'\beta}))}{1+\exp(\alpha_{2} + \gamma(\boldsymbol{x_{S,tijk}'\beta}))}

(10)

\displaystyle\mu_{S,ti,1}

\displaystyle=\big(\mu_{S,ti11,1},\mu_{S,ti12,1},\cdots\mu_{S,tiJK,1}\big)^{T}

(11)

\displaystyle\mu_{S,ti,2}

\displaystyle=\big(\mu_{S,ti11,2},\mu_{S,ti12,2},\cdots\mu_{S,tiJK,2}\big)^{T}

(12)

\displaystyle\mu_{S,ti,3}

\displaystyle=\big(\mu_{S,ti11,3},\mu_{S,ti12,3},\cdots\mu_{S,tiJK,3}\big)^{T}

(13)

\displaystyle\mu_{S,ti}

\displaystyle=\big(\mu_{S,ti,1}^{T},\mu_{S,ti,2}^{T},\mu_{S,ti,3}^{T}\big)^{T}

(14)

\displaystyle\partialderivative{\mu_{S,{tijk},1}}{\alpha_{1}}

\displaystyle=\mu_{S,{tijk},1}(1-\mu_{S,{tijk},1})

(15)

\displaystyle\partialderivative{\mu_{S,{tijk},2}}{\alpha_{1}}

\displaystyle=-\mu_{S,{tijk},1}(1-\mu_{S,{tijk},1})

(16)

\displaystyle\partialderivative{\mu_{S,{tijk},3}}{\alpha_1}

\displaystyle=0

(17)

\displaystyle\partialderivative{\mu_{S,{tijk},1}}{\alpha_{2}}

\displaystyle=0

(18)

\displaystyle\partialderivative{\mu_{S,{tijk},2}}{\alpha_{2}}

\displaystyle=\mu_{S,{tijk},2}(1-\mu_{S,{tijk},2})

(19)

\displaystyle\partialderivative{\mu_{S,{tijk},3}}{\alpha_{2}}

\displaystyle=-\mu_{S,{tijk},2}(1-\mu_{S,{tijk},2})

(20)

\displaystyle\partialderivative{\mu_{S,{tijk},1}}{\beta_g}

\displaystyle=\mu_{S,{tijk},1}(1-\mu_{S,{tijk},1})x_{tijk,g}\text{ for }g=1,\cdots,q

(21)

\displaystyle\partialderivative{\mu_{S,{tijk},2}}{\beta_g}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\mu_{S,{tijk},2}(1-\mu_{S,{tijk},2})-\mu_{S,{tijk},1}(1-\mu_{S,{tijk},1})\bigg)x_{tijk,g}\text{ for }g=1,\cdots,q

(22)

\displaystyle\partialderivative{\mu_{S,{tijk},3}}{\beta_g}

\displaystyle=-\mu_{S,{tijk},2}(1-\mu_{S,{tijk},2})x_{tijk,g}\text{ for }g=1,\cdots,q

(23)

\displaystyle\partialderivative{\mu_{S,ti}}{\alpha_{1}}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\partialderivative{\mu_{S,ti11}}{\alpha_{1}},\partialderivative{\mu_{S,ti12}}{\alpha_{1}},\cdots\partialderivative{\mu_{S,tiJK}}{\alpha_{1}}\bigg)^{T}

(24)

\displaystyle\partialderivative{\mu_{S,ti}}{\alpha_{2}}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\partialderivative{\mu_{S,ti11}}{\alpha_{2}},\partialderivative{\mu_{S,ti12}}{\alpha_{2}},\cdots\partialderivative{\mu_{S,tiJK}}{\alpha_{2}}\bigg)^{T}

(25)

\displaystyle\partialderivative{\mu_{S,ti}}{\beta_g}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\partialderivative{\mu_{S,ti11}}{\beta_g},\partialderivative{\mu_{S,ti12}}{\beta_g},\cdots\partialderivative{\mu_{S,tiJK}}{\beta_g}\bigg)^{T}\text{ for }g=1,\cdots,q

(26)

\displaystyle\bm{Y_{tijk}}

\displaystyle=(I(Y_{tijk}=1),I(Y_{tijk}=2),I(Y_{tijk}=3))^{T}

(27)

\displaystyle\bm{Y_{i}}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\bm{Y_{i111}^{T}}\\[6.0pt]
\bm{Y_{i112}^{T}}\\[6.0pt]
\vdots\\[6.0pt]
\bm{Y_{iTJK}^{T}}\end{pmatrix}

(28)
